# PPU function-boundary audit — GT6 BCUS98296 v1.00

Generated by `tools/boundary_audit.py` from `work/bin/EMAIN.elf` and `work/analysis/emain/functions_final3.json` (OPD: `work/analysis/emain/EMAIN.functions.json`).

This is a static audit. It does not rebuild the port and does not modify `gt6/src`, `gt6/generated`, `third_party` or `gt6/build`.

## Summary

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Functions before | 64,134 |
| Functions after | 70,975 |
| Net change | +6,841 |
| OPD starts (< code end) | 33,090 |
| Ends corrected | 3,425 (2,579 trimmed, 846 carried into a merged prologue) |
| New starts added | 8,310 |
| Starts removed / merged | 1,469 |
| OPD starts lost | 0 |
| Warnings | 0 |

Code range: `0x00010000`..`0x00F908C4` (16,255,172 bytes).

6,588 OPD entries had their corrected end below the OPD upper bound, releasing 10,929,780 bytes of container span (the code is redistributed into the added entry points below, not deleted). The ledger only records the subset that also differs from `functions_final3.json`; this count compares directly with the OPD end.

## What was wrong

The candidate list treats *next candidate start* as *function end*. That is safe only when the next candidate is the next real function. GT6 has three failure modes that break that assumption:

1. **OPD containers.** `EMAIN.functions.json` ends every OPD entry at the next OPD start. Where the OPD table is sparse, one entry spans many real functions. Example: `0x163174..0x16E3F8` contains **159** separate 0x120-byte functions; the candidate list gives the first one a 45,360-byte body.
2. **Leaf/thunk followed by a frame function.** A 12-byte `li; li; rldicl; b` thunk followed by `cmpwi; mflr; stdu` cannot be split by the lifter's prologue recovery, because the second function's `mflr` is before its first `stdu`. Example: `0x10ED8` (12 bytes) merged with `0x10EE8..0x110A4`.
3. **Prologue anchors.** `find_functions.py` sometimes seeds the `mflr` of a prologue, not the real entry, when a `mfcr`/`cmpwi`/`lis` setup instruction precedes it. The corrected list moves the start back. Example: `0x14398` (mflr) -> real entry `0x1437C` (mfcr; stdu...).

The corrected list partitions every candidate range and every uncovered gap into maximal CFG-reachable islands, detects relative jump tables, keeps `bctr` switch containers whole (splitting only at unambiguous prologues after a terminator), and then merges prologue anchors into the real entry.

A positive delta in the ledger is **not** new code. It happens when a range such as `0xB4EE80..0xB4EEE0` is only the prologue of a larger function: `0xB4EEE0` was a false `mflr` start. The real function starts at `0xB4EE80` and runs to `0xB57230`. The ledger records the earlier start's end as extended and the later anchor as removed; together they are one function.

## Representative corrections (byte-verified)

| case | old range | corrected range | first insn | last insn |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPD container split | 0x00163174..0x0016E3F8 | 0x00163174..0x001632C8 | cmpwi cr7, r5, 0 | b |
| leaf thunk split from next frame function | 0x00010ED8..0x000110A4 | 0x00010ED8..0x00010EE8 | li r4, -1 | b |
| prologue-anchor merge (start moved back) | 0x00B4EE80..0x00B4EEE0 | 0x00B4EE80..0x00B57230 | mfcr r12 | blr |
| missing switch dispatcher added | not in candidate list | 0x00AF1758..0x00AF17E4 | cmplwi cr7, r4, 0x18 | .word |
| start moved from mflr back to mfcr/stdu | 0x0001437C..0x00014398 | 0x0001437C..0x00014428 | mfcr r12 | blr |
| nop padding start removed | not in candidate list | 0x008B33B8..0x008B33DC | lwarx r9, r0, r3 | blr |

## Confidence

| Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|
| high | End stops on an unconditional transfer (`blr`/`b`/`bctr`/`rfi`) and the next instruction is a new entry, **or** the start is an OPD start, a `bl` target, or a prologue with no competing evidence. |
| medium | Gap-resident code island that is not directly referenced by a `bl`/branch; still a valid code region, but provenance is weaker. |
| low | Range contains `bctr` / an unrecognised computed jump, or the trim is blocked by OPD/`bl` evidence. Not auto-split; needs a manual look or the lifter's jump-table pass. |

**Interpretation.** Intentional multi-entry `memset`/`memcpy` family. The overlapping starts at 4-byte offsets are real alternate entry points into a shared body; the body uses computed (`bctr`) jump tables. Do not collapse these aliases. The audit leaves them intact and keeps each `bctr` container whole. Only the canonical entry ends at the next 4-byte alias; the outer ranges share a common end.

The corrected list keeps the dispatcher/table/cases whole and preserves the explicit case aliases. `0xA305B8` is a case target not in the candidate list; the lifter's jump-table pass discovers it, and the audit leaves the container intact rather than inventing a partial body.

## The 0xA13644 record (correct as-is)

The earlier `docs/STATUS.md` / `docs/HANDOFF.md` claim that `0xA13644` is an 8-byte `lbz r3,0x10(r3); blr` is **false**. Ground truth from `EMAIN.elf`:

```
0xA13644  stdu  r1,-0x90(r1)
0xA13648  mflr  r0
0xA1364C  std   r29,0x78(r1)
0xA13650  lis   r29,0x165
0xA13654  std   r28,0x70(r1)
0xA13658  addi  r29,r29,-0x5510      ; 0x164AAF0
0xA1365C  std   r0,0xA0(r1)
0xA13660  addi  r28,r29,0x60         ; 0x164AB50 (lock object)
0xA13664  clrldi r28,r28,0x20
0xA13668  mr    r3,r28
0xA1366C  bl    0xA42DE8             ; lock
0xA13670  addi  r3,r29,0x90          ; 0x164AB80 (flag object)
0xA13674  bl    0x3C0E84             ; lbz r3,0x10(r3); blr
0xA13678  mr    r29,r3
0xA1367C  mr    r3,r28
0xA13680  bl    0xA42D3C             ; unlock
0xA13684  ld    r0,0xA0(r1)
0xA13688  ld    r28,0x70(r1)
0xA1368C  clrldi r3,r29,0x38
0xA13690  mtlr  r0
0xA13694  ld    r29,0x78(r1)
0xA13698  addi  r1,r1,0x90
0xA1369C  blr
0xA136A0  stdu  r1,-0xA0(r1)         ; next function
```

`functions_final3.json`'s `0xA13644..0xA136A0` is therefore **correct**, and this audit does **not** shorten it or list it as a correction. The 8-byte getter is `0x3C0E84..0x3C0E8C` and was already correctly listed. The fake-leaf body patch in `gt6/generated` has been reverted. Keep the first-instruction cross-check as a general gate, but do not special-case `0xA13644`.

## Suggested post-lift patch policy

Cases that cannot be fixed by ends alone:

1. **Never patch a generated body without checking the ELF bytes at the function's start.** `0xA13644` is the canonical counter-example: a former manual patch labelled it a "corrected leaf getter" although the bytes are a frame-allocating wrapper. The patch has been reverted; keep the first-instruction check so this class of edit cannot return.
2. **Multi-entry routines** (memset/memcpy family at `0xA4FD30..0xA4FF30`, jump-table cases, `0xA305B0`, `0xA56F64`, `0xAF2658`): keep every explicit entry, give each an end that stops at the next entry or the shared epilogue, and do not let a tail entry inherit the next OPD function's end. The lifter's `_MAX_MID_TAIL` cap is a safety net, not a boundary.
3. **`bctr` switch containers** (`0xA30568`, `0xA56C04`, `0xA4FDBC`): do not CFG-split them. Add the dispatcher and, if needed, each case target as an explicit start whose end is the shared epilogue. The lifter's jump-table pass already discovers relative tables; explicit entries are for cases reached only through function-pointer tables.
4. **Prologue anchors:** prefer moving the start back to the `mfcr`/`cmpwi`/`lis` setup instruction over patching the body. The real entry is the first instruction after the previous function's terminator, not necessarily `mflr`.
5. **OPD containers:** keep the OPD start and `toc`/`opd` metadata, but let the CFG-derived child starts bound the container. An OPD end is an upper bound, not a size.
6. **Semantic bugs** (the boot flag, null constructor entries) belong in `gt6/src` or HLE stubs, not in generated bodies. A body patch hides the fact that the guest never initialised the object.
7. **CI gate:** run `python tools/boundary_audit.py --check` after every lift. It fails on: lost OPD starts, a start that is not code, an end past the OPD bound, or a generated function whose first guest statement does not match the ELF.

None.
